Now each present tells at the fire how it fared that day (gathered kJ in per cent of its own need, thin, thirsty, cold, and whether it changed its ways lately), and a body in trial sums, night after night, the days of the mates **who changed nothing** (`Trial::heard`); at `trial_days` it compares its own days with their mean (integers, the division whole, [`Lately::better_than`] unchanged: kept if better on one count and worse on none), and falls back on its own days before **only when it heard nobody** (alone at the camp, all in trial, itself out). Same land, same weather, same season: what is common cancels out, the way remains. A perception learnt by listening, never by reading the world; it can be wrong (a teller lucky that day). `News::Tried` says against how many heard days over how many nights, or against itself. The content's `trials` values are unchanged; `--no-fire` (the vivarium) gives trials without any hearsay: nobody copies, every judge is against itself.

  - **Amended on 23 November 2026 (lot 4, PR 1 b) — the will is innate and acquired, and the lessons heard weigh the choice.** The project lead, reading the twenty-year kept ways (every way kept and contradicted in pairs, 128 to 158 trials a way and a tribe): "les essais sont censés arriver avec la volonté… c'est certaines personnes précises qui vont avoir une volonté plus élevée. Et la volonté, elle est à la fois innée, mais aussi acquise… la plupart des gens sont frileux… très peu de gens sont des aventuriers"; and "les leçons d'historien : ça a marché, ça n'a pas marché… ça rentre dans la mémoire collective et les autres arrêtent de le reproduire". Until now the will was drawn flat from 0 to 100 and never moved, and a will of 0 still tried 2 % of its days. **Innate**: the will is drawn from the seed and the body's id as before, but read on a skewed table of percentiles (`trials.will_innate`: the 80 % most timid between 5 and 20, the next between 20 and 40, the last 7 % up to 90: one or two adventurers in twenty-five); a child's the same way, no inheritance (the souls' question, open). **Floor at zero**: `try_percent_by_will` starts at (0, 0), a will of 0 never tries. **Acquired**: a kept trial raises its body's will by `will_step_kept` (5), an abandoned one lowers it by `will_step_lost` (5), within 0 to 100: a trait moved by one's own experience, a gesture, not an outcome. **Lessons**: every trial told at the fire (D55), kept or given up, is held by the listener as an access; when the listener chooses its next trial, each candidate step (wider, narrower, out or home, more store, less store, the home moved) starts at a weight of 100, and each heard trial **of the same change** (the same way, the same direction) adds `lesson_weight` (50) if it was kept and takes it away if it was given up, whole for `lesson_days` (50), half for as long again, nothing after, scaled by how much a body of this will heeds the lessons (`lesson_heed_by_will`: the timid whole at will 0, the adventurer 30 % at will 100), and never under `lesson_floor` (10): **a weight, never a ban**, what failed for one may work for another. The way is then drawn by weight from the seed, the id and the day (`brain::lessons`, `start_trial`); the kept way heard still comes first (D55). `--no-trials` keeps the content's will table (the fire's credit reads the wills) and sets every chance to none. Placeholders in `content/needs.ron`, section `trials` (D30), each explained there.
  - **Amended on 23 November 2026 (lot 5b, PR 1 bis) — the will tempered.** The project lead: the will stays innate, drawn at random, not inherited ("pour l'instant… pas le moment"); the fixed steps (a kept trial +5, an abandoned one −5, about one trial in four kept) wore everyone down: no adventurer left after twenty years. Four rules, each a perception of oneself ("je suis d'accord avec toutes tes solutions"), placeholders in `content/needs.ron`, section `temperance` (D30): (1) **temperance**: failures in a row are counted; the first `bears_by_innate` cost nothing (2 for the most timid, 6 for the boldest: a bold one bears more), each past them costs `failure_milli` (a point) times its rank past them; (2) **a success weighs more the later it comes**: it forgets the streak and gains (n + 1) units for a streak of n, the unit computed from the rarity of a kept trial (`kept_percent`, 25) so that **average luck gains on average what its failures cost** (integer sums, `Temperance::success_milli`); all failures, it loses faith; luck, it asserts itself; (3) **the temperament comes back**: each season the will closes `relax_permille` (83 ‰) of its gap to the innate will, half of it in two years; (4) **being listened to gives confidence**: half a point (`listened_milli`) when another chooses to join its call at the fire (not a child taking its mother's decision) or copies a way it told. The will is kept in thousandths (`Mind.will_rest`); `Mind::will` stays its points. Absent, the section leaves the fixed steps of before (`will_step_kept`, `will_step_lost`), which the vivarium's control and the tests keep. The same four rules hold a confidence by activity: D56, amended the same day. Measured over twenty years (two tribes of 25, seed 1, cadence 1, PR 1's binary and content against PR 1 bis's, one run each on a shared machine): the mean will of all who lived 15.5 at the start, **7.8 at the end with the fixed steps, 18.2 tempered**; a will of 40 or more at the end 2 / 9 (the bold of tribe B 3 in its first year, 3 to 6 in its last five); trials 274 / 493 judged, 64 / 76 kept (23 % / 15 %); alive 63 / 55, dead 30 / 32 (30 of illness each), births 43 / 37; gathered 502.0 / 460.8 million kJ (−8 %); the food carried at noon, the mean of the forty tribe-years, 2,600 / 1,239 thousand kJ (tribe A alike, 1,021 / 1,083 a year; tribe B's halved: more trials, fewer kept, its stock of ways lower). One seed: the ten-seed series says whether it holds.

  - **Amended on 23 November 2026 (lot 5b, PR 1 bis, after its causes) — the slow ways judged on their horizon.** The project lead: "il faut les deux corrections… et garder bien entendu la volonté tempérée", and the slow ways judged over their natural horizon rather than five days. A trial of a way named in `trials.horizon_days` lasts that many days and is judged on as many, its own and the mates' heard (`Trials::days_for`; placeholders: `stock_kj` 12 and `sleep_out` 12 days, a season of the vivarium's fifty-day year); the other ways keep `trial_days` (5), never fewer; a mind keeps twice the longest of them (`Trials::longest_days`). Why: a stock or a night at the camp changes nothing in five days of gathering, so keeping or dropping one was a coin toss, and the tempered will, trying twice as often, tossed twice as many (seed 1 of the causes: tribe B's stock 7,500 against 178,125 kJ in PR 1, and the reverse on the other seeds). Measured in D56.
  - **Amended on 23 November 2026 (lot 5b, PR 1 ter) — the horizon counted in seasons.** The project lead validated it ("je suis tout à fait d'accord, c'est un bel ajout"), then fixed the home: a home tried alone is a short stay, then back if not kept; a season's horizon is for the acts of a group. `trials.horizon_days` becomes `trials.horizon_seasons`, (way, seasons), converted by the calendar (`seasons × days_per_year / 4` in whole days, `Trials::days_for`, `Trials::longest_days`); placeholders `stock_kj` 1 and `sleep_out` 1, twelve days of the vivarium's fifty-day year, the same as before. `home` stays unnamed, so a lone home trial keeps `trial_days` (5). Why: a horizon in days is tied to one year's length; in seasons it follows the calendar, and a longer year lengthens the slow ways with it. The acts of a group do not exist yet: their season comes with them.
  - **Amended on 23 November 2026 (lot 5b, choosing where to live) — home goes where the body lived best.** The project lead: "ils doivent se souvenir des endroits où vivre et choisir eux-mêmes"; "Ce n'est pas quelque chose qu'on veut coder, on veut quelque chose qui réfléchit soi-même. Mais il est absolument essentiel qu'ils trouvent l'endroit optimal où vivre"; and the rule of every trial: "j'ai essayé, ça a marché, je le fais; à force de répéter ça ne marche plus, je change". A home trial no longer steps a day's walk towards the plants: it goes to the place the body itself slept at whose notes are the best (D53, the night's notebook), slept at for at least the nights its will asks (`trials.home_nights_by_will`, three for the timid, one for the bold). With no such place better than its home, it draws another way that day. `trials.new_home_walk_tiles`, `trials.far_home_from_will`, the direction drawn and the edge's margin go. A body hungry **or thirsty** at nightfall now counts a day of distress. The judge, imitation by hearsay (D55) and the fire's decisions (D56) are unchanged. **On 24 November 2026** (the lead: "ajouter les endroits de la journée au carnet"), the first run having shown notebooks holding only the camp where everyone sleeps, the place may also be one it drank or gathered at in its days: "I walk far every day to drink or gather there; what if I lived there?" **Then the same day, the grade made one** (the lead: the home must end at the optimal place, for good, "pas juste les prémices"; no ordered list of criteria): a place is graded by how the body would fare living there, every perception in its own gauge's unit and counted alike, per mille of a day of that need: what its grove gave (food gathered, water drunk, each up to a day's worth) less what its nights left lacking (hunger and thirst at nightfall, cold at dawn) and what walking cost; a place never slept near takes the home's nights, their walk less what living there would save. A new perception (fear, warmth kept, sight) is one more `brain::Sense` and enters the grade by itself. **The bold search for better (24 November 2026**, the lead's idea b): with nothing wrong, a body may still start a trial, by `trials.calm_try_permille_by_will` (none under will 40, 2 % a day at 100), judged the same. **The night's cold follows the season (24 November 2026**, the lead): `needs.cold_season_percent` scales `cold_per_day_at_night` (spring 74, summer 32, autumn 63, winter 100, in proportion to a temperate lowland's mean nightly minimum under about 20 °C), so only winter nights fill the gauge; the world's climate by place is not read yet. **Walking costs energy (24 November 2026**, the lead: "que la marche coûte de l'énergie"): a body pays `needs.walk_j_per_m` (130 J a metre for a grown body, about 2 J per kg per metre above rest, a smaller body by its size) for every metre it walks, on top of the day's need; thirst from effort is unchanged.
  - **Amended on 24 November 2026 (lot 5b, PR 2 a) — one home at a time.** The project lead: "c'est important qu'il ait une notion : maintenant c'est ma maison, ce n'est plus celle-là. C'est là où il va revenir." A body has **one home** (`Ways::home`): the place it returns to at dusk, sleeps at (unless it sleeps out) and whose fire it sits at. A home trial sets the new home at once, provisional until judged: kept, it is the home, and the old one survives only as a memory in the notebook (`Mind.lived`); not kept, the trial's ways before set it back and the body returns. No other home exists anywhere in a mind: `home_at_dusk` is only that home as it was at nightfall, the fire the body lies down for while the dawn's vigil may move the home.
  - **Amended on 24 November 2026 — the innate will from the run's seed.** The project lead ordered it fixed: the innate will is drawn from the run's seed and the body's id (salt `TRIAL_WILL`, the same skewed table), no longer from the map's, which gave every body the same will whatever the vivarium's `--seed`; in the game the world's seed is the map's, so nothing changes there.
  - **Amended on 24 November 2026 — the brain's daily draws from the run's seed too.** The project lead agreed: every draw of the brain (whether to try, the calm trial, the way tried, copying a way and losing a step of it, the look and the reputation at the fire, the credit given to a teller: salts `TRIAL_TRY`, `TRIAL_CALM`, `TRIAL_WAY`, `TRIAL_COPY`, `TRIAL_COPY_WAY`, `TRIAL_COPY_LOSS`, `FIRE_LOOK`, `FIRE_REPUTE`, `FIRE_CREDIT`) now comes from the brain's seed, the run's, no longer from the map's, so two runs on one map live other days. The world's own draws of life (conception, a child's sex, death in childbirth, the daily death) stay on the map's seed: moving them would put a run seed in the world and its snapshot, left for later.
  - **Amended on 24 November 2026 (lot 5b, PR 2 b) — a move is called at the fire, each one decides.** The project lead: no vote, "sur les 20 personnes qui l'écoutent, il y en a 15 qui sont d'accord et qui y vont… plusieurs individualités". A home trial drawn from the body's own notebook (`lived_home`) is no longer moved at nightfall: it is held (`Mind.calling`) and the body lies down at its old camp, so that at the dawn's fire it can say "I go to live at X, who comes?" (`Act::Moves`, D56). Its trial starts then, or alone if it was at no fire. Each one who joins opens **its own** home trial toward X (`Trial.caller` names whom it followed), judged by the world alone like any other trial: each can come back. A home heard at the fire is **no longer copied by `imitate`** (the copy by prestige was the camp moving on someone else's word); a kept home heard still comes first when the body tries (`heard_candidate`), as a trial judged as any. When the body says stop on its way to a home beyond a day (D56, the held goal), the move is given up as a trial is: the home goes back, the will learns it. Reason: the lead's rule that the collective comes from individual decisions, each judged by its own body, never from a group rule; and "one home at a time": home is where it comes back.
  - **Amended on 24 November 2026 (lot 5b, PR 2 b) — a home trial judged by its grade over a season** (the project lead, 24 November 2026: "il faut vraiment que le déménagement apporte quelque chose": the bodies better their standard of living and move only to a place that is really better, bringing over the medium and long term more food, more children, more of everything). A home trial was judged on five days by the strict test (`Lately::better_than`: nothing worse, one thing better), the moving and settling days among them, so it failed almost always (the previous control run: all 16 of a move on day 40 came back after five days, the caller's notes saying +139). Now a home is tried over **a season** (`trials.horizon_seasons` names `home`, one season, 12 days of the vivarium's year, as `stock_kj` and `sleep_out`) and judged by **the same grade that chose it** (`brain::fare`): the new home's, by the body's own nights and days there, against the old home's by its notes (`brain::home_grades`); kept when the new beats the old by the margin (a hundredth of a day). Never slept at the new home, or the same place, given up; no notes left of the old home, it counts nothing. The other ways keep the old judge. The moving day enters the new home's notes as any day: within a day's walk the body sleeps there that night, so the night's cold and company are its first night there, and what it gathered on the way is noted at the groves where it gathered; the notes fade a twentieth a day, so the season outweighs the first days. `News::Tried.grades` tells both grades (`people[].trials[].grades_permille`).
  - **Amended on 24 November 2026 — a move is a consequence, never drawn.** The project lead, 24 November 2026: "Faut pas du tout que ça soit un but. L'idée, c'est une amélioration, une évolution… Le déménagement est une conséquence, pas une cause", and "une personne qui arrive dans un endroit et se dit : ici, c'est tellement mieux, c'est ici qu'il faut venir"; the synthesis of the three thinkers, validated that day (`content/ai-reserve/review/vivarium/lot-5/synthese-penseurs-demenager-2026-11-24.md`, "Le mécanisme proposé", points 1 to 7). **The home leaves the trials**: `Way::Home` is no longer a candidate of `brain::lessons`, nor a way heard (`heard_candidate`) or copied; the will still drives the other trials, the bold search for better and the wish to go and see. **A move is called** (`brain::call_move`, at nightfall, held for the dawn's fire as in PR 2 b) only by a grown body with a place it lived **surprises** at (D53 amended: the days its will asks, `trials.home_nights_by_will`, its spouse's days counting by `moving.spouse_day_percent`) whose grade beats its home's, season against season, by the margin once the move is repaid, **and** that feels **pushed** here (its recent days worse than its usual days of the season by `moving.push_permille`), or whose surprise there was **huge** (`moving.huge_times` the threshold). A home trial is then judged as in PR 2 b, by the same grade, now season against season (`brain::season_grades`). Reason: a move drawn by chance was a search, not an answer; a move must come from what the body lived here and there. Placeholders in `content/needs.ron`, section `moving` (D30); absent, nobody moves home (`--no-moves`, the vivarium's control). Measured (two tribes of 25, five years, seeds 9, 1 and 2, with and without `--no-moves`): alive 82, 83, 72 with moves against 85, 85, 85 without; births 37, 40, 30 against 40, 40, 40; no death of want either way; 16, 20 and 50 homes moved, 4 to 19 % given up after their season, no return A, B, A by a new move within a year; the movers had about as many hungry nights as the stayers over the season after on seed 9 (0.56 against 0.50), fewer on seeds 1 and 2 (1.25 against 1.65, 0.48 against 1.92); the cost was couples apart at the dawns of conception (seed 2: 448 against 37). With these placeholders, moving does not make the population grow faster: left to the project lead.
  - **Amended on 25 November 2026 — a floor under every trial.** The project lead: "0,1, c'est rien et ça peut ne pas compter, mais effectivement, dès qu'on a mis des multiplicateurs, au lieu d'avoir 5 fois 0, on a 5 fois 0,1. Donc je pense qu'il faut le garder en permanence pour tout." Every body, calm or in distress, whatever its will and a will of 0 included, starts a trial of some way on a journée with at least `trials.try_floor_permille` (1 ‰, a D30 placeholder in `content/needs.ron`): a blind trial below will 40 is possible, rarely; above the floor nothing changes (the same draws). The extra chance of a heard deed (D57) multiplies from the body's own chance, or from this floor when its will gives less: one floor for all.
  - **Amended on 25 November 2026 (with D57, the knowledge lot) — "I could not try" is not "it did not work".** The project lead approved the audit `content/ai-reserve/review/vivarium/lot-5/audit-invention-savoir-2026-11-25.md` (proposals 1, 2, 4 and 6): "Non, c'est parfait, c'est même incroyable… C'est très très bien ce qu'ils ont suggéré. Je suis partant, on y va." A trial of the hands in whose days no gesture its hands did not know at its start could be begun, nothing new being within reach (or no idle hour), **is not judged**: the ways stay as they were, no `Judged` is held for the fire, so no lesson is told, the will learns nothing, and the vivarium counts no failure (`News::Untried`, not `News::Tried`). Before, each was a lesson "it did not work", told to everyone, and these hundreds of false lessons pressed the weight of trying one's hands down to the floor (`lesson_floor`) in every listener and wore the will of the founders. A trial in which a gesture new to the hands was begun and gave nothing is judged and given up as before. How the other ways are judged is unchanged.
  - **Amended on 25 November 2026 (the life lot) — a stock kept is judged over three seasons.** The project lead, approving the audit of life and population (`content/ai-reserve/review/vivarium/lot-5/audit-vie-population-2026-11-25.md`, proposal 4): "Non, c'est parfait, c'est même incroyable… C'est très très bien ce qu'ils ont suggéré. Je suis partant, on y va." `horizon_seasons` of `stock_kj` **1 → 3** (`needs.ron`, content alone). The horizon is both the trial's length and the window it is judged on (`Trials::days_for`: seasons × the cycle's days / 4, a season 16 days of the 64-day cycle; the judge compares the body's days over it with those heard of camp-mates who changed nothing). What is kept in autumn feeds only at the end of winter and in spring, the lean weeks; judged over one season, a store begun in autumn was judged before it could pay. Over three seasons (48 days), a store begun on any day of autumn is judged through the next spring. **Decided alone, told:** three rather than two (two would end at winter's end for a store begun on autumn's first day, before the spring); the cost: a body in that trial tries no other way for 48 days, and a mind keeps twice the longest trial's days, 96 instead of 32.
  - **Amended on 25 November 2026 (the clocks audit, principle F) — a short trial is judged within one season.** The project lead chose principle F of the clocks audit (`content/ai-reserve/review/vivarium/lot-5/audit-horloges-2026-11-25.md`, §2 point 4, §3 row F): **a trial is judged over a span where its effect can show, without the change of season deciding it.** **The measured reason**: a short trial (one judged on `trial_days`, 5 journées, a third of a 16-journée season) heard nobody often enough to be judged against its own 5 journées before, and one begun near a season's end was judged on the next season against the last: the season decided, not the way. Trials judged in the first 5 journées of a season against those judged later: hands **2 kept of 83** against **23 of 178**; search width **6 of 23** against **2 of 21**; home 17 of 24 against 10 of 19 (the home is judged by its grade over a season, unchanged here). The effect goes both ways: noise of the season.
    - **The rule.** A short trial **begins only when its days end within the season of its first day** (begun on journée *d* of a season of *S*, only if *d* + `trial_days` ≤ *S* − 1: journées 0 to 10 of 16); otherwise no trial begins that day (the draw is not turned to another way) and the body tries again on a later day by its usual chance, in practice the next season. **Its days before**, the judge's other side when it heard nobody, **are taken in that season only**: up to `trial_days` journées ending on its first day, never before the season's first journée, their counts scaled to the trial's days as the heard days already are (floor); within the season the window is exactly as before. A trial of the hands whose days restart when a deed is learnt (D57) restarts on that journée **but never later than the season's last journée less `trial_days`**, so it too is judged before the season turns. The judge against camp-mates who changed nothing over the same journées is unchanged (the season cancels there).
    - **Not touched**: the ways judged on their horizon in seasons (`sleep_out` and `home` one season, `stock_kj` three, #367) keep their windows; a season shorter than a trial (never in v1) leaves the old rule. The store counting the hearth's share (#373) acts only on `stock_kj`, a horizon way: no interaction with this rule.
    - **Trials per season** (computed, not run: a body always in distress, a daily chance *p*): today a short trial and its judging night take 6 journées, so at most 2.67 a season; now at most 2 (journées 0 to 10 for starting). At *p* = 2 %, 5 %, 10 %, 30 %, 100 %: 0.30 → 0.22, 0.65 → 0.48, 1.08 → 0.81, 1.92 → 1.56, 2.67 → 2.00 a season, **about a quarter fewer short trials** (74 to 81 % kept); the slow ways unchanged.
    - **Decided alone, told**: (1) the gate rather than a judgment held back: a trial straddling the turn is never begun, so no trial is wasted unjudged; (2) a way drawn that cannot fit is not replaced by a slow one (the draw stays the same in every season); (3) the short before window early in a season is scaled to the trial's days (1 journée counts for 5 on the season's first day), the precedent being the heard days; (4) the gate is read from the calendar (`season_of`), so the vivarium's 64-journée cycle and the legacy 50-journée one both hold. Not verified on runs (the lead chooses them). No protocol, storage or dependency change.
  - **25 November 2026 (D09 amended)**: `lesson_days` is written `lesson_seasons`, 4 (64 days in calendar v1).
  - **Amended on 26 November 2026 — the worth of a place halved at a walk, not at a distance.** The project lead, 24 November 2026: "Faut le passer non en mètres, mais en temps de marche"; the plan `content/ai-reserve/review/vivarium/lot-5/cerveau-temps-de-marche-plan-2026-11-24.md`, its seven decisions validated on 26 November 2026 as recommended (decisions 1, 2 and 7). The way `worth_half_tiles` (32 tiles, some 2 game minutes on the reference ground) becomes `worth_half_minutes`: a plant's worth is halved at a **walk of 30 game minutes** (`WORTH_HALF_MINUTES`; a game minute is a 1,440th of the game day, 11.25 s of calendar v1's), where the walk there and back takes the hour of gathering it buys (`thought.plant_choice_hours`): 483 tiles on the reference ground, 321 in the forest. The walk is read straight, at the pace the body felt at its last nightfall on the ground it lay on (`Mind::walk_milliminutes`, the clock of D53 amended the same day, shared with the point of no return); the real terrain is not read (decision 2: later, apart, only if forest camps show the gap). Trials and copies double, halve or meet it in minutes as before. Wherever the worth was weighed, it is weighed so: the place to gather, the plant in it, the evening's choices (whose own pace scaling it replaces: the pace is in the felt walk), what is told at the fire and the rank in the notebook. The vivarium's data say `worth_half_minutes` and `median_worth_minutes` (`meta.format` 2); the lab reads both. The cost accepted by the project lead: "if they go farther, it bothers me, but it's OK: the goal is that they explore the map more and more" (26 November 2026). The four rules (this one, the notebook and the water of D53, "near" of D56) land together. Nothing stored, **no protocol change, no storage change, no dependency**; `plan/` untouched.
  - **Amended on 26 November 2026 — a lived problem is carried in mind, tried at a free moment and judged by it (with D57).** The project lead, on the discovery study (`content/ai-reserve/review/vivarium/lot-5/etude-decouverte-2026-11-26.md`): "not a feeling added to push them, rather a limitation that leads to thought: my arms are full, I can't take more…"; he approved as "perfect" the study's variant B on one problem, "arms full", with a control flag; then, on the first build (PR #382), "one may think about a problem in front of it, or later; and in front of it, it is often when one is free", and the judge given a season. Reason: a trial was born of a global distress and the hands were judged by the food of five journées, which a folded bark does not move in five journées (seed 5, 960 journées: 6,649 trials of the hands, 241 kept, 15 free draws), while the world refused a gathering with the arms full 1.2 times per person-journée, 46 % of them at full hunger, and nothing remembered it. The first build (trial begun only on the spot, given up after three returns, about two journées) gave on seed 11 over 192 journées 69 trials, 0 kept, 52 untried.
    - **The problem** (`sim::brain::problems`): a refusal of the world, lived, carried by the body as an entry of its things to think about: its kind, the place, the journée, how many times. One kind today: `full`, a gathering stopped full (`WorkEnd::Full`, so the plant still bears) with no container holding anything and the arms full. The refusal is linked to the property of the materials its test read, `holds_g`, **in the content** (`content/needs.ron`, section `problems`, D30 placeholders), never in the code; no word for what would answer it (D57). A problem not lived again for `fades_seasons` (2) is forgotten, as the lessons fade. **Amended on 27 November 2026 (D57, step 2 of "acts and discovery")**: the link to `holds_g` is removed from the content and the code: the study of that day verified that no decision read it, and it named the answer. `full` is lived when a gathering stops full with the arms full and nothing it carries lying in a hollow; a hollow merely carried holds nothing. Its judge is unchanged: the limit moved when a later gathering stopped full finds the body carrying more than its arms alone, which now takes food **put** in a hollow. A kept trial of the hands leaves known only the deeds whose product served (D57).
    - **The things to think about — the project lead's design, for lightness.** Each body keeps a short list of the problems it lived, at most `mind_slots` (3): a problem lived is **pushed on top** (already there, it moves up and its count grows: no duplicate); a full list lets its oldest drop off. Living it, with no trial nor call under way and the season's trials from it not spent (`trials_per_season`, 1), the chance of its **will** may make the body **mean to try it** (the distress percent or the calm per mille, at least the floor of every trial; salt `PROBLEM_TRY`, D24). It begins a trial of its hands **there and then** if it is free and the things around allow a gesture its hands do not know; else it waits. **A free moment is any calm moment (the lead's rule); not only the evening**: a moment the brain already passes through — any thought its hands reach, by day or in the evening (no need called it: not drinking, eating, sleeping, looking for food, walking home, nor night), and the nightfall's point where a calm trial may be drawn, neither special nor excluded — at which a free body **pops the top problem it means to try** and begins the trial if something new lies around; with nothing new, the problem goes **back below the top**, and the body looks again only beyond the hands' reach of there or another day; never spent untried. **Free**: hunger and thirst under the lines the day's distress counts (`HUNGRY`, `THIRSTY`). The list is read only when a problem is lived and at those free moments, and only when not empty: **empty, it costs nothing; nothing scans or draws for problems by tick**. A problem not lived for `fades_seasons` (2) is dropped when the list is touched. The gesture is then drawn among what the things around allow, as in any trial of the hands: **nothing weighs it toward the answer** (the study's variant C is refused). The existing guards hold: one trial at a time, `tries_per_day`, the temperance of the will. (Seed 11, 192 journées, two tribes of 25: a build whose only free moment was nightfall, the will drawn there, gave 23 trials, 0 kept, the first container on journée 78, carriers 0 / 18 / 19 at the cycles' ends; with every calm moment, this rule, 78 trials, 4 kept, journée 33, carriers 17 / 16 / 22. The restriction to the evening was the build's, never the lead's.)
    - **Judged by its problem, over a season.** Kept as soon as a later gathering stopped full finds the body carrying more than its arms alone (when its hands learnt something), as "what served is kept". **Given up** when `gives_up_seasons` (1, D09) passed since it began with the problem lived again and the limit unmoved, its ways as before. Either way the will learns. A trial in which nothing new was tried is **not judged** (`end_untried`); one whose time is out with the problem never back **lapses** unjudged, nothing told, the will untouched; nothing else changes that night, as after any trial ended.
    - **Told with its problem.** The trial's record at the fire carries its problem (`Record::Trial.problem`): "problem X, deed Y, it worked". Only a listener who lives the same problem takes it as a deed heard (the extra chance of D57), a kept way (D55) or a lesson; one who lives it and begins a trial from it aims first at the deeds told with it. A listener without the problem has no reason to try it.
    - **The control** (`--no-problems` in the vivarium, the content's section replaced by none): behaviour is bit-identical to the code before this change (seed 11, 48 journées, two tribes of 25, `vivarium-wide`: the same sha256 of the JSON without `meta.timing`, the new `problems` block and the two new content factors `problems.*`).
    - **Measured** (`docs/10`, block `problems`): the problems by kind, per 1,000 person-days and lived hungry; the times a body willing to try waited; the trials begun on the spot or at the nightfall's free moment, on a tale, kept, given up, untried, lapsed; the first container's journée; the carriers and the mean will at each cycle's end; the free draws of the hands.
    - **Decided alone, told**: (1) "the arms full" is the vivarium's own count `full_arms`: stopped full, no container holding, the food at the arms; a load full of objects with the arms not full is not that problem; (2) "free" is read from the gauges at the day's distress lines, not from the calm path of trials (`distress_days`), which counts whole days; (3) "something new" is a gesture the things allow whose deed its hands do not know, deeds told included only as gestures it could draw; (4) the will is rolled when the problem is lived, until it means to try it, never at a free moment (thoughts are many a day); the season's trial is counted when it begins; at nightfall a problem trial begun comes before the other trials; (5) the short trial's season gate does not apply: the trial spans its season; (6) kept also needs something learnt by the hands; (7) a kept trial told with its problem weighs the lessons only for listeners carrying it; a list read at the fire is not touched, so an entry may outlive `fades_seasons` until the list is next touched (at the latest the trial's end). Not saved (the minds live only in the vivarium): **no storage nor protocol change**.
  - **Amended on 27 November 2026 (D59) — two problems felt, the gut and the chest.** The list of things to think about holds two more kinds, `ill_gut` and `ill_chest` (property `felt`: lived by the body, read by no material), pushed by an ailment lived or seen in a kin, never tried from in this first step (`trials_per_season: 0`). They share the three slots with `full`, so an ailment can push `full` off a body's list. The rest is D59.
  - **Amended on 28 November 2026 (with D57) — a trial of the hands keeps only a lived problem relieved, and ends once.** The project lead, on the audit of the trials (`content/ai-reserve/review/lot-5/audit-essais-2026-11-28.md`): a success is having relieved a lived problem, not a gesture that gave a thing (D57, amended the same day: seen is not kept). For the trial's ending this changes two things. **(1) Every ending of a trial of the hands keeps the chain that served and nothing else**: kept by its problem, given up, lapsed, judged by the days, or cut short by a move followed, which before put the hands back as before the trial and left the deeds heard as an aim with no trial. **(2) The judgment of a trial from a problem is pushed by `trial_days` at most once past its time** (`ProblemAim::last`), however many deeds it sees; before, each deed learnt pushed it again, and a trial of a season lasted 33 journées in the run of seed 41. **The guard of four gestures a journée** now bounds the whole work of a trial that has found no answer yet: before, it bounded the draws only, and the deeds a trial had just learnt were done again without limit. **Still open** (the audit's rule 5, not decided): a novelty heard at the fire still opens a trial of the hands to a listener who lives no problem (D57, 25 November 2026), which the amendment of 26 November above ("only a listener living the same problem has a reason to try") says only of a kept trial told with its problem.
  - **Amended on 28 November 2026 (second round, with D57) — one verdict for a trial of the hands.** The project lead validated it on a case of the run of seed 42: person 57 filled a hollow 43 times during a trial begun on a tale, the trial was judged not kept on journée 160 (`kept: false`, the will learning a failure, the tale told as a failure), and the chain was kept the next day as "not its own" (`own: false`). Two judges answered the same question: the trial's days (`judge_trial`, `problems::judge`) and the chain (`keep_what_served`).
    - **The rule.** A trial of the hands is **kept if and only if a chain of it served its lived problem** (`handwork::keep_what_served` now answers it). Then the will learns a success, the deeds are its own (`News::DeedKept.own` is always true), and the tale told is a success. Every ending agrees: judged by its days (`handwork::judge_trial`), judged or lapsed by its problem (`problems::judge`, which replaces `lapse`), cut short by a move followed (`handwork::cut_short`, from `Brains::start_move` and `go_to_live`), or by another trial.
    - **Not kept** is as before by its ending: judged with its problem lived again, given up and the will learns a failure; lapsed or cut short with no chain that served, it ends unjudged and the will learns nothing.
    - **Decided alone, told**: a trial ended beyond the arms by a deed kept before it is still given up when no chain of *this* trial served (as before, noticed, not changed).
    - **Measured** (`docs/10`, `hands.work`): `trials_kept`, `trials_not_kept`.
  - **Amended on 28 November 2026 (third round, with D57) — how a trial of the hands begins, weighed against what the body already knows.** The project lead refused the audit's rule 5: a novelty heard at the fire still opens a trial of the hands to a listener who lives no problem ("a deed heard is tried more readily, yes"), which closes the point left open in the first round above. What changes is what a tale can open (D57, amended the same day: a known result is not tried again): the deeds whose result the listener knows, and that brought it nothing, are no aim; a tale with nothing else opens no trial (`brain::weigh_tale`), whether it comes by the extra chance of a deed heard or as a kept trial heard that its will would try (`start_trial`, which then draws a way as if nothing had been heard); a trial from a lived problem still begins by its problem, its aim alone sifted (`problems::begin`), and now also begins, with nothing new around, when the body remembers a deed that answers a problem and can work its chain here. The chances and their rolls are unchanged.

### D51 — The vivarium runs fast: the world's step costs what moves
- **Reason**: the project lead, 22 November 2026: "on va faire tourner beaucoup de runs… de plus en plus au cerveau… il va falloir des runs beaucoup plus rapides". The runs are the vivarium's judgement (D41, D50): a run of two tribes of 25 over 40 days took about a minute, and the brain's lots to come each ask for pairs of them. Lightening is measured first (`perf`, call stacks on a frame-pointer build), and a change that alters a result is wrong: the results must be the same before and after (D24).
- **Consequences**:
  - **The invariant.** A change is checked by the pair of the vivarium's runs (`--world vivarium --people 15 --children 7 --infants 3 --cadence 1 --seed 1 --tribes 2 --days 40`, with and without `--no-trials`): the `sha256` of each page **with its timing paragraph removed** (since lab 6, 23 November 2026, the long page: `--page full`; the default page is minimal, D41) (`<p id="timing">…</p>`, the one place the wall time is written) must equal the reference's. The hashes of 22 November 2026: `c23a897558b835eb…` (trials) and `5ee8345c42aafd3d…` (no trials), the same before and after this decision's changes, on the release build as on the profiling build.
  - **The terrain is made once a chunk and read in place.** Terrain is still generated from the seed, never stored as tiles (D31, unchanged): `Land` makes a chunk's tiles on first touch and holds them in a bounded cache (`TILE_CACHE_CHUNKS`, 6,596 chunks, about 8 MB: four views with their rim and the far view's chunks; the vivarium's 4,624 chunks fit, the game's valley of 9,216 does not, and its oldest chunks are made again when read, the same bytes). Counted over the vivarium's 40 days: every chunk's tiles made once (4,624, nearly all by the page's picture at the end, which reads the whole map), 1,712 chunks of woods, 1,432 of plants, 1,691 of trees, each once: nothing was made twice. What cost a quarter of the process was the **reading**: a thread kept its four recent chunks in a list searched and reordered at every tile, the chunk cloned out (`Arc`), and a path search in a window of 19 × 19 chunks fell past the four to the land's lock and its cache (two maps updated a read). A thread now keeps a **direct table of 16 × 16 slots** a kind (tiles, woods, water), a chunk's slot its place in that square, so chunks less than sixteen apart never share one; the tile is read in place. Bound: 256 slots × 3 kinds, about **1 MB a thread** at most, pinned past the land's caches. A walk's planning (`TrunkLookup`) likewise keeps the chunk of trunks it read last.
  - **Routes are not planned less.** Counted over 10 days of the same run: 14,362 departures (29 a body a day), 14,285 on a fresh order of the brain, 255 (1.8 %) toward the target already held; 11,172 searches of **37 tiles expanded on average** (budget 1,500). Planning less would change the routes, so the results; the cost was each tile read, now cheap. `Routing::route_from`, 25 % of the first profile, is the continent's water routed at birth (`plan::relief`), not a body's route, and is left as it is.
  - **Measured** (release, the pair alternated with the reference on a loaded machine, so the gain is read within a pair): run **58.9 → 46.1 s** and **67.9 → 49.8 s** (−22 to −27 %), the birth unchanged (13 s); CPU of the whole process 76.8 → 63.8 s and 86.9 → 67.1 s. Per body-tick (288,000 ticks, about 50 bodies): about 4.1 → 3.2 µs. Shares of the process: `Land::terrain` with its recent list 13.7 → 5.8 %, `TrunkLookup::blocks` 3.7 → 2.5 %; `gen::noise` 8.6 → 10.3 % of a shorter whole (the chunks' drafts, once each, mostly for the picture). What remains, measured for the next step: the brain at 38 % (the plants placed as the minds learn them, 19 %: about 6 ms a chunk, already held once; `crops::text_key` hashing a name through a fresh `Vec` at each call, 3 %; the known plants' map, 6 %), the walks' planning at 20 % (`Map::skirt` 4 %, the search itself), the birth at 17 % (the continent, which the server keeps in SQLite, D28, and the `vivarium` binary bears anew).
  - Protocol and storage unchanged; no dependency added; `plan/` untouched.
  - **Amended on 22 November 2026 (lightening 2: the brain, nothing visible changes).** The brain audit of the same day (`content/ai-reserve/review/vivarium/allegement/audit-cerveau-2026-11-22.md`) read the brain at 65 % of the loop after the terrain's lightening, three items making three quarters of it: the plants placed as the minds learn them, the choice of the plant to gather (every known plant re-read in the world by name), and the plants seen again and rejected at every look. The lead agreed to the lot that changes nothing visible, under the same invariant: the pair of 25 a camp (`--people 25 --tribes 2 --days 40 --cadence 1 --seed 1`, with and without `--no-trials`) hashes the same, page without its timing paragraph (`04330094958504db…`, `5b9efeb9013ffd3b…`) and JSON without `meta.timing`, after each of eight commits. **S1** the brain asks what is ripe by the crop's number (`World::ripe_of_crop`) instead of two names compared against the 46 crops, and a kind's name is hashed as its bytes come, no vector (20.7 million calls a run, 50 million vectors). **S2** a look computes once the rank of the plant its full memory keeps last (`memory_bar`) and learns only what outranks it: ranks are total, so the set remembered is the same (1,364 plants a look were pushed and rejected). **S3** the plant to gather is chosen once a thought. **S4** a chunk's bearers are held once, the brain reading the world's own list with two bytes a bearer for its kind's number; a bearer's kind is a shared `Arc<str>` made once a kind a chunk, written as text so the snapshot's JSON is unchanged (D28); the trees around are read in place, not copied. **S5** the due minds are noted during the one sweep that stays (the gauges), and only the minds with chunks left unseen are read for them. **S6** the brains' caches of bearers and shores widen to the world's chunks (`Lru::widen`, the vivarium's 4,624; 1,764 was four views and two tribes of 60 overflowed it on day 19), held once with the world's. **Measured** (release, reference and new alternated on a loaded machine, so read within a pair, CPU time of the process): run CPU of the pair, machine quiet, three alternations agreeing within 0.4 s: **31.9 → 26.5 s** with trials and **31.7 → 26.2 s** without (**−17 %**, the birth's 7–8 s unchanged in both); the loop's two sections by the audit's probe (the same timers on both builds, two runs each agreeing within 0.1 s): `Brains::think` **15.18 → 11.36 s (−25 %)**, `World::step` 8.75 → 7.95 s, the brain's share of the loop 63 → 59 %; inside the brain, the choice of the plant 3.90 → 1.85 s (S1, S1 b, S3), the plants pushed for ranking 54.2 → 9.1 million and `remember` 1.52 → 1.00 s with the learning 2.08 → 1.11 s (S2, S2 b), the sweeps 0.79 → 0.75 s (S5); placing a chunk's bearers 6.24 → 6.09 s (S4: the cost is `flora::plants_in`, intrinsic, left for a later lot or S11); peak RSS 146 → 140 MB (S4 held the bearers once, but they are not where the memory is). Over 250 days on a loaded machine, 313 → 200 s of CPU, the page the same (`9ca080882d1eaf56…`). Two steps were measured first and done again: S1 b hashes a crop's kind once with the content (`World::crop_keys`, not stored) instead of at every read of a plant; S2 b reads the bar before searching the memory and compares worths through products, not quotients (`Bar::admits`, checked against the rank by a test), because the first S2 saved in `remember` what it spent in the learning. S4 keeps `Bearer.kind` a text in the snapshot's JSON (D28): the audit's `u16` would have changed the storage. S6 changes nothing on the pair, which never reached the cap, and changes the pages of two tribes of 60, which did. Not done: the behaviour steps of the audit (S7–S13: deciding before looking, the plant chosen once an hour, patches instead of bushes, hunger hysteresis, a shorter plant sight, the worth's distance), each a rule for the lead.
  - **Amended on 22 November 2026 — a chunk's bearers placed ahead of the bodies, on helper threads** (`sim::ahead`; the lead's yes to the parallel study's alternative (a)). Placing the trees and plants bearing a crop of a chunk (`flora::plants_in` over the trees of nine chunks, a few milliseconds a chunk, once a chunk) was 39 % of the brain at the audit of the day, asked on the simulation thread the moment a body looked at new land. The placing is a pure function of the map, the seed and the content, immutable under a world, and its result is memoised in the world's shared `Ground` where the server's threads already read it (D29). So a `Placer` of `--helpers N` plain `std::thread`s (default 1; 0 is the simulation thread alone, today's path) is told every 16 ticks where the bodies stand, how far they see and where their routes end (`Placer::follow`, a bounded channel the simulation never blocks on), and places first the chunks of each body's sight widened by two chunks (`LEAD_CHUNKS`) and of its route's end, nearest first, each once, skipping what is held. The simulation thread asks the same cache and finds them placed: **the same bearers, placed ahead or placed now, by construction**; a late helper changes nothing but the time, the simulation thread placing the chunk itself as before. The helpers decide nothing, write nothing a rule reads, and the simulation never waits for them: workers of the one simulation thread (D29), not a second simulation. The vivarium also widens the world's ground caches (trees, rocks, bearers; `Ground::widen`) from four views (1,764 chunks) to the world's chunks, as the brain's own were (S6), with or without helpers: the bodies see more land than four views over a long run, the helpers fill the caches with the land around the bodies, and a chunk's bearers read nine chunks' trees. The server keeps its caches as they are (the valley has 9,216 chunks; widening them is about 4 MB of trees, a decision of its own). The lab (`tools/lab`) passes `--helpers 0` when it runs several runs side by side: each helper takes about a fifth of a core more, which a series spends better on another run. Measured on a quiet machine (load about 3, 0 and 1 helper alternated, two passes within 0.1 s), the pair of 25 a camp over 40 days with trials: the loop **32.7 / 31.4 s → 24.7 / 24.8 s (−23 %)**, the simulation thread's own CPU 42.5 / 41.1 → 34.5 / 32.4 s, the process's CPU 43.2 / 41.8 → 51.2 / 49.0 s (+18 %: about a thousand chunks placed ahead that no body looked at, and the helper placing at about 9 ms a chunk beside the simulation); the widened caches alone give −6 %. Over 250 days with trials: **187 → 93 s (−50 %)**, of which the widened caches alone give 187 → 97 s: at 1,764 chunks the trees' and bearers' caches thrashed from day 59 on (5,670 placings for 4,380 chunks explored, each placing its nine chunks' trees again), and the helper adds −4 % on top. Pages and JSON (without the timing paragraph and `meta.timing`) identical with 0, 1 and 2 helpers on the 40-day and 250-day pairs (before the rules of D53: `b8ac93618fff39cb` / `46ddf3150d9bbd31` over 40 days, `d4e0faa12105f69b` / `2e400ed36006eda2` over 250, the values of #315; after them, the 40-day pair with 0 and 1 helper again alike). With D53 a body sees plants no farther than `plant_sight_tiles` (30), and the helpers follow that sight. The timing paragraph and `meta.timing` tell the helpers and the chunks placed, in all and ahead. Not done, for the lead: the server's caches, and a wider lead or a helper pool for the game's server, where a view's chunks are the bodies' to come.
  - **Amended on 22 November 2026 (lightening 4: the world's step, nothing visible changes).** The world's step audit of the same day (`content/ai-reserve/review/vivarium/allegement/audit-pas-du-monde-2026-11-22.md`) read the step at 43.5 % of the loop after lightening 2, routes 76 % of it, and the A* exhausting its budget of 1,500 tiles to say "no way in sight" before a river: 85 % of the tiles expanded, some 40 % of the step. The lead: "à part S12, on peut absolument toutes les faire". The seven steps that change nothing visible, one commit each, the pair of 25 a camp hashing the same after each (page without its timing paragraph `b8ac93618fff39cb…` with trials and `46ddf3150d9bbd31…` without, JSON without `meta.timing` `16f9c04e583a97e2…` and `161c8902b249c082…`, as main's), and 250 days (`d4e0faa12105f69b…`, JSON `a7d16127c1ee5831…`) and the pair with children and infants (`8f2205b11118b18c…`) the same too. **S1** the connectivity pre-test by chunk before an A* (`foule/chemins.md` §2, PLAN A2, never coded): each chunk's walkable pieces (`ground::Links`, a label a tile, four-connected, under the movement rule's terrains and the trunks; 2 KB a chunk, held by the `Ground` per chunk, version and walkable mask) joined across the chunks' edges of the sight square by a union-find (`TrunkLookup::may_join`); a target no piece joins, whole chunks and all, is refused with no tile expanded, the budget counted as spent as a search at its budget is; "maybe" runs the A* as ever. A test compares old and new over random pairs on the game's valley and the vivarium's zone. **S2** the A* arena a thread (`map::Arena`): a flat square of the sight, each slot stamped by the search that last reached it, nothing zeroed, the open set kept; the same expansion order, ties and path. **S3** the skirt's five windows likewise (`map::SkirtArena`), the sweeps reading the cells marked, sorted into the square's order. **S4** a straight walk's sidesteps answered by a flood of the 9 × 9 square (the bits of one word) before the small A*, whose "no" the flood gives exactly. **S5** the bodies grow once a day (`World::grow_due` raised by every change the growth reads: a body added, carried, set down, orphaned or dead, a pregnancy, the life rule, the content, a load) and the carriers' state is built only when some body is carried. **S6** the gatherer keeps its crop's number (`Work::crop`, never stored), asks rate and `ripe_of_crop` by it, shares `gives` (`Arc<str>`, written as text: the snapshot's JSON unchanged), records a pick in place and adds the grams to the load. **S7** the vivarium's continent kept on disk (`vivarium::continents`): a cache of the vivarium tool, nothing of the game's (the server keeps its continents in `continents.sqlite`, D28, untouched), the bytes of `Continent::to_bytes` under `${XDG_CACHE_HOME:-~/.cache}/soul-sim/continents/continent-<seed>-<side>-g<generator>.bin` (60 MB, `std::fs`, no dependency), read back by the next run of the same key, on by default for the vivarium's world alone, `--no-continent-cache` to bear it anew; the same pages either way (a test, and D28's amendment of 19 November). **Measured** (release, the machine loaded by other agents all day, load 4 to 8: reference and new alternated, the probe's two passes read at their minimum, shares read within one run): the pair's process CPU with trials **37.8 → 20.0 s** (two alternations: 37.8 / 47.3 against 20.6 / 20.0, about −47 %, the birth's 5.5–6.5 s gone to 0.3 s in it); the loop by the probe (the same timers on every build) `World::step` **12.06 → 5.63 s (−53 %)**, `Brains::think` 15.1 → 12.1 s (the same code: the noise of the day), the step's share of the loop 44 → 32 %, the loop −35 %; the step commit by commit, S1 12.06 → 7.92 s (−34 %: the pre-test), S2 → 6.76 (−15 %), S3 → 6.69, S4 → 6.88 (within the noise of ±0.2 s), S5 → 6.04 (−12 %), S6 → 5.78 (−4 %), S7 → 5.63; the birth **5.9 → 0.32 s** (S7: the continent read in 0.3 s instead of born in 5.5 to 13 s by the load), peak RSS 132 → 122 MB on the pair; 250 days 429 → 175 s of process CPU under load, the page the same. Protocol and the game's storage unchanged; no dependency; `plan/` touched by a test-only re-export, the ignored `plan::net` tests run by hand (one fails on main already, `SeaAsLand` on two marsh windows, the same two on the branch). Not done, by the lead's word: S12 (the page's picture) refused; S8 is the brain's; S13 (needs and walks by event) later; the behaviour rules S9–S11 are D54's.
  - **Amended on 25 November 2026 — the vivarium holds its whole land, and keeps it on disk between runs (nothing visible changes).** The project lead, the same day: "On ne peut pas faire des runs de 1 heure, c'est pas possible"; he agreed to keep the terrain in a file. Measured first (`perf`, frame pointers, `--world vivarium-wide --tribes 2 --people 25 --days 144 --seed 1`, richness 1.0): **69 % of the simulation thread was making chunks of land** (tiles, woods, water: `Land::tiles`, `Woods::draft`, `Window::waters_of_chunk`), path search 17 %, the brain 9 %, the rest of the step 4 %, the report 2 %. At richness 1.0 the bodies and the helper placing ahead reach some 17,500 of the wide map's 18,496 chunks, while `Land` held 6,596 (`TILE_CACHE_CHUNKS`, sized for four views): the chunks were let go and made again, over and over, each some milliseconds. Two changes, the land's bytes unchanged:
    - **The whole map held.** The vivarium widens its map's caches of tiles, woods and water to the map's chunks (`Map::widen_land`), as it already widened the ground's (trees, rocks, bearers). Each chunk is made once a run. Memory: about 3 KB a chunk, some 55 MB on the wide map, the run's peak 0.26 → 0.41 GB. The game's server never widens: its caches stay bounded (D37).
    - **The land kept on disk** (`vivarium::lands`). A chunk's tiles, woods and water are a pure function of the world (D24): at the end of a run, before the picture, the chunks it holds are written (`Map::land_bytes`: a magic and layout version, the map's size, each chunk, a checksum) to `${XDG_CACHE_HOME:-~/.cache}/soul-sim/lands/land-<world>-g<generator>-<program>.bin`, merged with what another run kept there meanwhile, and the next run's map takes them before its first tick (`Map::take_land_bytes`), reading them as made. The picture's terrain (the 512 × 512 points the report's map reads, which make every chunk of the map: some 3.5 minutes on the huge world) is kept the same way beside it. **The file is named by the program's fingerprint** (its executable hashed), not by the content: the land reads no content, so the content's variants of a series share one file, and the land's code changes far more often than `VIVARIUM_GENERATOR` is raised, so a file is only ever read by the build that wrote it; a new build makes its land again on its first run. Files of other builds untouched for three days are cleared. `--no-land-cache` makes every chunk anew. A local file of this tool, not the game's storage (D28): the storage schema is untouched.
    - **Measured** (the same run, release, pinned to two performance cores, alternated, load about 3): main **415 s**; the whole map held, no file **147 s**; with the file, the first run **157 s** (it writes 57 MB), the next ones **138 and 136 s**: **3.0× faster**. The report's data, timing apart, is the same byte for byte in all of them (`sha256` of the JSON without `meta.timing`, and of the page without its timing paragraph, equal to main's). `meta.timing` gains `land_made` and `land_kept` (chunks of tiles, woods and water made by the run and read from the file); the year's log line tells the chunks of land made.
    - Protocol and storage unchanged; no dependency added; `plan/` untouched.
  - **Amended again on 25 November 2026 — the trees and plants kept on disk, the helper's choice and the skirt made cheaper (nothing visible changes).** The project lead the same day, on the three ideas left after the land: "c'est vraiment parfait, c'est là-dessus qu'il faut aller", toward runs of 100 age-years (800 days). Measured on the same run with the land kept, a third of the chunks' bearers were placed by the simulation thread itself (16 % of it), the helper spending half its time choosing again, at each of the thousand hints a second, among every chunk around every body in a sorted map; and the skirt's flood of the obstacle (`Map::skirt`), its bank marked tile by tile through a closure for each obstacle tile and each tile's ground asked once per obstacle tile beside it, was a fifth of the simulation thread. Three changes, each checked by the same hashes as the land's:
    - **The trees and plants kept with the land.** The trees of a chunk as the seed placed them and the trees and plants bearing food (`Ground::to_bytes`, `Ground::take_bytes`) are written at the end of a run beside the land and taken before the next run's first tick. They read the content (species, open biomes, flora, crops), so their file is also named by the content's fingerprint (`Placing::fingerprint`, FNV-1a of their debug text): a run of another content places its own. About 39 MB on the wide map. A run that finds them places no chunk: the helper and the simulation thread idle on it.
    - **The helper's choice** (`ahead::Chooser`): the chunks done a bit a chunk of the map, the nearest distance a slot a chunk stamped by the hint, only the chunks left sorted; the same chunks in the same order as `ahead_of`, those done and those off the map dropped (a test pins it); two helpers share the chunks by their place on the map. The helper's own time drops; it is still outrun when the bodies reach new land faster than a chunk is placed (a run with no kept plants still has a third placed by the simulation thread), which only the kept file answers.
    - **The skirt's flood** reads the square by row and column, marks the bank a row at a time, and asks each tile's ground once (`looked`, a seventh array of the arena): the same cells marked, in the same order.
    - **Measured** (release, pinned to two performance cores, alternated old and new with no other run on the machine; the laptop throttles at 90 °C, so the instructions are given too): 144 days **115.0 → 80.3 s**, instructions 2.08 → 1.04 × 10¹², CPU 200 → 84 s; 320 days **179.7 → 147.4 s**, instructions 3.06 → 1.80 × 10¹². The gain is in the first year (57 → 33 s), when the bodies reach new land; later a year costs about what its living cost in both (17 to 20 s a year for 27 alive), path search 57 % of the simulation thread, the brain 23 %. The report's data and page hash the same as main's at 144 and 320 days.
    - Protocol and storage unchanged; no dependency added; `plan/` untouched.
  - **Amended a third time on 25 November 2026 — the land prepared ahead and shared across builds, the trees placed once and kept (nothing visible changes).** The lightening audit of the day (`content/ai-reserve/review/allegement-mesures/audit-allegement-3-2026-11-25.md`) found the land's file almost never read in the series (named by the whole program's fingerprint, it died with every pull request, and the seeds of a series start together: 6 of 37 runs read it), and a chunk's trees placed again at every item laid, lifted or picked in it (115,638 placings for some 15,000 chunks over 400 days, 6 to 12 % of a run). The project lead the same day: on the land, "Pour le 1, je te laisse regarder s'il y a même moyen de l'optimiser, mais oui, je suis carrément partant", and he accepted that a file written by another build be read, protected by a check of 30 chunks; on the trees, "il faut trouver une solution pour arrêter de les charger une fois là où ils sont, et puis c'est tout… il faut regarder quelques techniques et quelques solutions".
    - **The files named by the land-making code, checked when read** (`vivarium::lands`). The land's and the picture's files are named by `land_code`, a hash of the sources of `sim` that make tiles, woods, water, the continent and the trees (`map.rs`, `land.rs`, `gen*`, `woods.rs`, `relief.rs`, `geology.rs`, `trees.rs`, `open_ground.rs`, `plan/*`, embedded in the vivarium's program); the trees and plants' file by `ground_code` (those, `flora.rs`, `ground.rs`, `ahead.rs` and the widest spread of a tree) and the content's fingerprint as before. A change of the brain, of `lib.rs` or of `content.rs` keeps them. A test asks every new source of `sim` to be sorted (land, ground or neither). When a file is read, 30 of its chunks drawn anew at each run are made or placed by the run first (with the chunks around them their woods and plants read), then every chunk the run holds is compared with the file's (`Map::take_land_bytes`, `Placing::take_ground_bytes`, `sim::land::Refused`); one that differs refuses the whole file, which is made again, with a note naming the chunk. The check costs about 0.6 s a run (`meta.timing.land_checked`).
    - **The land prepared ahead, once for every seed and series** ("charger à l'avant"). A run whose world has no whole file (maps of at most 20,000 chunks: the vivarium's and the wide one) makes every chunk of the map before its first tick on all the machine's cores (the tiles, then the woods and water, then the trees, then the bearers, a pass each so no two threads make the same chunk), writes the files whole and moves them over, under a lock file: the runs of a series started together wait for the first and read its files. `vivarium --prepare-land --world W --content DIR` does the same and simulates nothing, for a series to call first. The land is the same for every `--seed` (the world's seed is the vivarium's, 14), so one preparing serves every seed and every series of the same land code and content. Measured: the wide world **16.8 s** on 16 threads (land 3.5 s, trees and plants 12.5 s), the vivarium's 5.2 s. A run reading a whole file keeps nothing more at its end (it rewrote some 100 MB before).
    - **The trees placed once and kept** (`sim::ground`). A chunk's trees, trunks and walkable pieces are held by the chunk alone, no longer by the chunk and its version: no change of men touches a tree yet (items taken or laid, crops picked), so the version moved them out for nothing. The version stays, for the server's frames. Felling, when it comes, lays a chunk's felled and planted trees over the seed's held there (a patch: some trees out, some in, never a new placing) and works that chunk's trunks and walkable pieces out again. The vivarium's ground also holds the ring of chunks around the map, whose (empty) trees and bearers the edge reads: held to the map's alone, they pushed the map's own out. Techniques weighed: (1) **kept by chunk alone, patched at a felling** — chosen, the least code, and the patch keeps a felling cheap; (2) a trees' own version, moved only by a felling or a planting — the same today, a felling then placing the chunk again whole (a few milliseconds), the fallback if patching proves awkward; (3) the trees only in the land's file — already done for the trees as the seed placed them, it did not stop the placings, the version taking each chunk out of it at its first change; with (1) the file serves all run long; (4) every tree placed before the run — done by the preparing above.
    - **Measured** (release, 160 days of the wide world, two tribes of 25, seed 1; each run on one performance core and its twin, four at once, so seconds are read within a batch; the load was high, the instructions are given): **no file (a series on a fresh build today) → the prepared file**: the first year **225–228 → 161–163 s**, two years 496–501 → 434–438 s (an earlier, lighter batch: year 1 at 124 s with the file); the audit's calmer machine read 156 → 79 s for the year. **The trees kept**: tree placings over the run **43,112–43,870 → 493–519** (those of the check), instructions **2.095 → 1.937 × 10¹²** (−7.5 %) and 2.050 → 1.963 × 10¹² (−4 %), the run 332.5 → 327.9 s and 568.6 → 560.2 s. The report's data, `meta.timing` apart, hashes the same as main's: the pair of 25 a camp on the vivarium (`--people 25 --tribes 2 --days 40 --cadence 1 --seed 1`, with and without `--no-trials`: `97e9306681e971f9`, `f2ef9c1a9e6bf4ca`, main's made fresh and the new one read from the prepared file) and the 160 days (`41477b7452fbb5e8`, five runs, main's and the new one's). `meta.timing` gains `land_checked` and `land_prepared_s`.
    - Decided alone, told: the fingerprint is of the sources, embedded in the program (about 1 MB), rather than of the program; `content.rs`, `crops.rs` and `lib.rs` are left out of it (changed by most pull requests; the check guards what they lend the placing); a run prepares the land itself when no whole file is kept, on all the cores for some 17 s, rather than only by a step before the series, so the lab and the series' scripts need no change; the check's chunks are drawn from the clock, which changes only the time; the preparing is limited to maps of at most 20,000 chunks (the huge world's file would weigh some 800 MB); the file is not read lazily (reading and checking it costs about a second a run, a lazy reading would save half of it).
    - Protocol and storage unchanged; no dependency added; `plan/` untouched.
  - **Amended on 26 November 2026 — lightening 5: the hands, the patches and the measures (nothing visible changes).** The lightening audit of the day (`content/ai-reserve/review/allegement-mesures/audit-allegement-4-2026-11-26.md`: where the time of a run growing to 200 and 400 people goes) ranked seven reductions that change no result; the project lead said yes to three of them the same day (the hearth's reserve is reworked apart, as heaps).
    - **The hands look once a thought** (`brain::handwork`, D57). A body lacking a tool or a material looked at the things around it once a deed tried, each look copying every record of food kept in reach (its material's text with it) and testing some 550 natural stones and branches tile by tile: 16 to 26 % of the run. It now looks once a thought, at the first deed that needs a thing, for the materials the deeds take up only (`World::things_near`, `items::natural_lies`): a natural rule of another material is not looked for, and nothing is copied. The thing found is the same, the nearest with the id breaking ties (a test pins the things seen against `World::items_near`).
    - **A patch's rows found once a square** (`brain::patch_grams`, D53 amended 25 November 2026). The sum of a small plant's patch searched the sorted plants nine times, once for each square around it. The three squares of a row lie side by side in the sort, so a row is found once, and once for all the plants of a square; the same plants are summed in the same order (a test pins it against a sum read plant by plant). Decided alone, told: the audit proposed to keep the patches by chunk and by version; what a patch sums moves with every tick (a crop ripens and falls a little each tick, D48) and with what each body reads (its sight, the plants a mate is at, those it left alone), so no key by chunk holds it; the search was made cheaper instead.
    - **Lighter measures** (`vivarium`). A page over `FULL_LINES_DAYS` (100 days) shows only the lines of a life, its trials and the fire, but every line of thought, meal, drink and gather of every person was kept to be counted at the end: such a line is now counted (`Person::unkept`) and kept only when the page shows it. The scarcity chain looks a mind up only when it reads it (a few ticks a day, not every tick); the food carried home weighs what a body carries only for a thought it counts (hungry, or eating).
    - **Measured** (release, the wide world, two tribes of 25, seed 5, 128 days, the land prepared; run side by side with the reference): the run **514.7 → 411.0 s** (−20 %), its peak memory 491 → 274 MB (the lines no longer kept); an earlier pair, not side by side and under unequal load, 676.7 → 418.6 s. The report's data is the same byte for byte (`sha256` of the JSON without `meta.timing`, and of the page without its timing paragraph, the long page too).
    - Protocol and storage unchanged; no dependency added; `plan/` untouched.

### D52 — The laboratory: series of runs created, followed and read from a local page
- **Reason**: the project lead, 22 November 2026, as the simulation phase opens: "on va bosser pas mal sur les simulations… des GUI qui nous créent facilement des versions… pages HTML, le plus simple… des comptes rendus, des tableaux, des courbes, des visuels… des comparaisons." A series of vivarium runs (D41) was launched from a JSON plan written by hand and read from an HTML synthesis found in a cache folder; the lead wants to ask a question of the simulation, launch twenty seeds with and without a rule, follow them, and read the answer, without a terminal.
- **Consequences**:
  - **A local tool in the repository, `tools/lab/`**, a small Python server on the standard library alone (`http.server`, `json`, `subprocess`, `threading`), serving on `127.0.0.1` only, opening nothing by itself (the lead opens the URL it prints). Its pages are plain HTML, CSS and a little JavaScript, in French, with no external resource: nothing goes outward, no dependency enters the repository, the game never runs it and depends on nothing in it. Three screens: **Nouvelle série** (a form: the question, the world, the people, the days with the year of 50 days shown, the seeds, with and without trials, the cadence with the warning that 10 is the game's and ten times slower, the runs at a time; "Relancer" a plan and "Dupliquer en changeant…"), **En cours** (every series with its live table, refreshed every two seconds from a JSON endpoint, a stop button with the confirmation built into the page, the final line and the links to the synthesis and to each run's page), **Lire / Comparer** (reports, tables, curves and comparisons between series).
  - **`soul-runs` absorbed**: the lab keeps the local launcher's plan format (`question`, `binary`, `content`, `out`, `runs` of `name`, `args`, `seeds`, `with_and_without_trials`) and its output layout (`<runs-dir>/plans/<name>.json`; `<runs-dir>/<name>/serie.json`, one `NAME.log`, `NAME.html`, `NAME.exit` per run, `synthese.html` at the end), so the plans already written keep working in both. The lab adds a `lab` key to the plan (the form's fields, the jobs) that `soul-runs` ignores.
  - **The runs belong to the lab process**: it holds their PIDs and ends them only on the user's button or when it exits; a series launched elsewhere is shown from its files and never stopped from here. The vivarium binary is not built by the lab: if it is missing or being rebuilt, the series waits and retries.
  - **In two pull requests**: the first builds the two first screens (this decision); the third screen comes once the vivarium emits its report as JSON beside the HTML page (the next pull request on `server/vivarium`), so that reading and comparing parse data, never pages.
  - **Amended 22 November 2026, after the first pull request** (the lead: "c'est toi qui dois pouvoir utiliser cet outil. Moi, ce que je veux, c'est comparer… tu fais de la modification, et du coup on relance les runs"). The lab is the AI session's tool to launch and the lead's tool to read and compare. Every series records its **version** in `serie.json` (the git commit and branch of the binary's worktree, "inconnu" without one, and a sentence of what changed), shown everywhere the series appears; a command line `lab.py run PLAN --change "…"` launches a series detached from any server; three **standard plans** live in the repository under `tools/lab/plans/` and are run after every change of the simulation; **comparing means first the same plan under two versions**, run by run (same seed, same with/without), seeds and with/without being the second control. The discoveries are read as a story of techniques per tribe, from the kinds the data names, never from a list in the lab.
  - **Amended on 23 November 2026 — the run page laid out (lab 5; the brief `content/ai-reserve/review/vivarium/lab/BRIEF-lab-5-layout.md`, two tiny tries read by the project lead: "ça va mieux… on va rester là-dessus").** The run page reads in five groups, each opened by its summary: **Résumé, Les gens, Où ils vivent, Ce qu'ils ont appris, Chronique**, then **Annexes**. A summary is never a paragraph: a verdict line, then points of a bold figure and half a sentence (4 to 6 at the top, 2 to 4 per section and per table, at most 15 words each), with an arrow and a small curve when the figure is a series in time; the points are chosen and written from the data, never by hand. Each tribe keeps one colour, a letter and a side of the map everywhere (tiles split A | B, tables, the chronicle's edge, the map). The tables show 4 or 5 columns with units and 10 rows, "plus de colonnes" and "voir les N" on demand, sort on click, and have no empty cell. The chronicle is read a year at a time, filtered by kind, the same events of a day grouped. Every name opens a card of the person's life (a strip of the years, trials, lessons, what they told at the fire). The map draws, under a year cursor, the presence, the camps dated and the places known that year, seen or heard. The heavy pieces come from the lab on demand, so the page itself stays light: `/morceau/<serie>/<run>?quoi=carte|annexes`, `/fiche/<serie>/<run>?id=N`, `/chronique/<serie>/<run>?an=N` (JSON), all read from the run's data file. Measured on the 20-year fire run of seed 1: the page went from 4.72 MB to 0.24 MB, the map coming at 1.87 MB, the annexes at 1.73 MB, a year of the chronicle at 0.06 to 0.33 MB. The charts and the map follow the dark theme. Still the standard library alone, no dependency, no external resource.
  - **Amended on 23 November 2026 — one page (lab 6; the brief `content/ai-reserve/review/vivarium/lab/BRIEF-lab-6-one-page.md`, a first step read by the project lead: "c'est déjà mieux", with three corrections to the home's cards).** The vivarium's page is folded into the run page, whose summaries stay first: **the factors** as cards by theme read from `meta.factors` (D41); **the maps** as two canvases side by side, each choosing among six kinds (terrain and relief, food by season, water, noons, routes and camps, deaths and births), sharing a year slider, pan, zoom and hover, their data loaded on demand; **the curves** as small multiples with one shared hairline; **food and tribes year by year** as big figures, trend points, a strip of the years with its remarkable years marked, the table folded; **the chronicle** opened by its trends and ten notable facts chosen from the data, one of each kind in turn; **ages and families**, a pyramid at the start and at the end and the families as folded cards. The home and the series pages are **compact cards**, closed by default (title, day, launch → end, runs, the purpose's first sentence or "but non écrit"), opened on a click for the verdict, what changed, the version and the runs; sorting, filtering, search and grouping by day. A plan gains `title` (the form's **Titre**; the standard plans have one). A series' aggregates and curves are grouped by what differs between its runs (trials, fire, or both), no longer by trials alone. The new pieces are `/morceau/<serie>/<run>?quoi=fond|carte-base|carte-an&an=N|courbes|relief|nourriture|annees` and `/api/resultats?serie=`. The run page weighs some 330 KB (the years table, the maps and the curves come on demand). Colours always come with a word or a sign. Reason: the lead reads one page per run and one list of series; the vivarium's long page and the lab's run page said the same things twice.
  - **Amended on 24 November 2026 — the pages in plain words (the project lead on the series page: "Il n'y a rien qui va"; the spec he validated the same day).** **The series page** is the series' question (`plan.question`), its answer under it (`plan.answer`, a sentence or two written when the series ends; "Réponse : pas encore écrite" until then) and **one table**, a row a run: at the start, births, deaths (of hunger), alive at the end, lived in all, couples together at dawn (`nights_apart`), the median distance from the camp at noon in spring (`positions` against `years[].camp`), homes changed (`home_changes`); a header's sentence explains each column. A row is named by the run's `note` (its seed added when the note does not say it), else by the settings that differ between the series' runs, and the seed. A run may name its **reference** (`ref`, `"series/run"`, another series' run as well): the references come first, marked, and each run's cells are coloured against its own reference, green better and red worse, the direction fixed per column (more births, alive, lived and couples together, fewer deaths), a gap of 5 % or less (2 at least, 3 points for the couples) left uncoloured as noise; the distance and the homes changed are never coloured, neither more nor less being better in itself. The series-wide means, the version and two-runs comparisons and the times line are gone; the computing time of each run (`meta.timing.wall_s`) is in small at the bottom, hidden when unknown. **The run page** opens with a breadcrumb (Labo › the series › the run) and a back button, one summary line (at the start, births, deaths by cause, alive, lived in all, the simulated days and age-years), then always the same parts in this order, each opened by one plain sentence: the map (the six maps and their year slider of lab 6), the population, births and deaths on a frieze, the ages (pyramids in age-years), the couples, the homes changed, the chronicle; the rest (the factors, the other curves, the years, what they learnt, the tables) is folded at the bottom.
